People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I guess I've missed a few calendar details here and there... I've had to double-check tasks that used to just be automatic for me. \n\nAs for the rest... I've pulled back from things. Like my photography outings, and mentoring. I just... I don't feel as present. Or reliable, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do these changes feel distressing to you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know if I'd use that word. It's just... how things are right now. I haven't really thought much about how I feel about it.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes been causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I think I just... I wouldn't really use that word. I'm not in distress. It's just... dealing with how things are right now. I haven't really dwelled on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Sleep is... it's been different, I suppose. There are nights where I'll only get maybe two or three hours. I'll just wake up way before dawn. But I don't feel tired. I just... get up and feel ready to start answering emails or organizing the appointment lists for the day. It just is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, would you say you’ve felt depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I haven't really felt down. Just... going about my day.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these changes cause you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I wouldn't call it distress. It's just... things are a bit off. Work is mostly fine, but I've... missed a few details on the calendar lately. Had to double-check things that usually just... happen automatically for me. And outside of work, I just haven't felt like doing much. I haven't gone out for photography in a while, and I... I stepped back from mentoring. I just don't feel like I'm really... there, or reliable right now. But I wouldn't say I'm distressed.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these changes have been significantly affecting your functioning day to day?\n\nPatient: Like I just said... not significantly, no. Work has stayed intact overall. I just... have to double-check those calendar details and tasks that used to be automatic for me. And... yeah, I've pulled back from the photography outings and the mentoring because I just don't feel as present or reliable right now. But I'm still functioning.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of feeling unusually euphoric, elevated, or overly energized?\n\nPatient: I guess there are times... stretches where I feel pretty upbeat. Like everything is just clicking perfectly. But... then something small happens. Like someone moves a meeting on the schedule, and I... I get kind of sharp with people. It surprises me, honestly. But yeah, sometimes I feel really energized.\n\nDoctor: When you have those upbeat, energized periods, how long do they usually last?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many days each one lasts, but... they've been coming and going for about eleven months now. Just these stretches where I feel really driven. I don't sleep as much during them, and... I guess I feel a bit too confident about things. But yeah... it's been recurring for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you feel unusually energized and confident, have you ever needed hospital care or caused major problems because of it?\n\nPatient: No... no hospital. I haven't caused any major problems. Just... just what I said before."
